Standard for minimizing max row sums in packing game: Player B chooses columns to minimize max row sums; Player A chooses rows to observe. Let's design an Adam optimizer in log-space for LSE objective with full conv weights. Pseudocode:
```
def lse_adam(a0, time_limit, T_schedule):
  n=len(a0); z=log(a); z -= log(a.sum())? softmax handles.
  m=zeros; v=zeros; t=0
  best_score=actual or surrogate
  for stage,T in schedule:
     lr = maybe 0.01
     for it in max_iters:
        a=softmax(z)
        b=conv(a,a)
        maxb=b.max()
        w=exp(T*(b-maxb)); w/=w.sum()
        # grad_a = 2 * weighted_cross(a,w) as above
        grad_a = weighted_gradient(a, w, n)
        # grad_z = a * (grad_a - dot(grad_a,a))
        g = grad_z
        t+=1
        m = beta1*m+(1-beta1)*g
        v = beta2*v+(1-beta2)*g*g
        z -= lr * m_hat / (sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
        # maybe renormalize z by subtracting mean to avoid drift? softmax invariant to shift; we can z-=z.mean()
        score=surrogate=2n*maxb_actual(a)
        if score<best: update
        # if no improve, break/restart
```

Let's consider **multiplicative gradient descent (mirror descent)** in a-space:
- a current sum=1.
- Compute weights w top peaks (softmax T b). grad_contribution c_i = Σ w_k a_{k-i} (without 2) or gradient/2.
- Update a_new = a * exp(-η c_i) / Z. This reduces coordinates contributing to peaks. Step η maybe 0.1-1.

Utility:
```
def normalize_sequence(arr):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0, 1000)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.ones(len(arr))/len(arr)
    return arr/s
```
But for internal score, sum=1. Output can be any scale? Objective invariant; choose sum=1 to avoid large. evaluate_sequence sum_a=1 >0.01. Values ≤1/n small <1000. Good.

`fast_convolve(a)`:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    n=len(a); size=2*n-1; m=1<<(size-1).bit_length(); A=rfft(a,m); return irfft(A*A,m)[:size]
```
For n=0? no.

`score_fast(a)`:
```
n=len(a); b=fast_convolve(a); return 2.0*n*b.max()
```
If a sum not 1, should scale: score invariant; if sum=1 okay. For safety, `score_fast(a): s=a.sum(); if s<1e-15 inf; return 2n*max(conv(a,a))/s^2`.

`evaluate_safe(arr)`: call evaluate_sequence with list floats clipped; handle exceptions.

`softmax(z)`:
```
z = z - np.max(z); e=np.exp(z); s=e.sum(); if s==0 return uniform; return e/s
```
To avoid underflow for large differences, max.

`log(a)`: `np.log(np.clip(a, 1e-300, None))`.

Now Adam LSE implementation details:
```
def optimize_lse_adam(a0, time_limit, K=512, T_schedule=None, lr=0.1, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, eps=1e-8, patience=2000):
    n=len(a0); M=nextpow2(3*n-1); a=normalize(a0); z=log(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a)
    # Maybe center z
    z -= z.mean()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=0; t=0
    for T, iters in schedule:
       t_stage=0; last=best_score; no_improve=0
       for it in range(iters):
          if time > limit or time - t0 > 1800 margin: break
          a=softmax(z)
          A=rfft(a,M)
          b=irfft(A*A,M)[:L]
          maxb=b.max()
          sc=2*n*maxb
          if sc < best_score - 1e-12: best...
          # weights
          # For numerical stability: z_pb = T*(b-maxb); if min z_pb < -745, exp underflows to 0 okay.
          eb = np.exp(T*(b - maxb))
          s = eb.sum()
          if not finite or s==0: eb top...
          w = eb/s
          # optionally zero out below K to reduce gradient cost? FFT all anyway; but if many underflow, fine.
          v=w[::-1]; V=rfft(v,M)
          grad_a=2.0*irfft(A*V,M)[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]
          # grad_z = a*(grad_a - dot(grad_a,a))
          gr = dot(grad_a,a); dz = a*(grad_a - gr)
          # Normalize dz to unit RMS
          dz_rms = sqrt(mean(dz*dz)+eps)
          dz = dz/dz_rms
          t +=1
          m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*dz
          vhat = beta2*vhat? Let's name vmax
          mhat = m/(1-beta1**t)
          vhat = vmax/(1-beta2**t)
          z -= lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
          z -= z.mean()
          if sc < best_score - 1e-12: no_improve=0 else no_improve+=1
          if no_improve > patience: break stage
    return best_a
```
But this optimizes LSE not actual; actual best may occur at intermediate. We track best actual/sc. If actual worsens, we still continue; z may wander. We can include **momentum reset** if score for many steps > best*(1+tol). 
Maybe use **rejection with annealing** to avoid diverging: If current score > best_score * 1.05, revert z to z_best and reset m,v, reduce lr? We can store z_best. Since we track best_a and maybe best_z. When best actual improves, save z. If a long time no improvement and current score much worse, revert to best_z. This can escape? Reverting prevents exploration but ensures stable. We can do: maintain `z_base` at best; every stage, start from best_z. For each stage, use Adam from best_z; if no progress, try different T/lr from best. This is more local.

Better: Use **annealing with restarts from best**: For each T in schedule, run a certain number of iterations starting from current best. If improvement, update best; if not, stop stage. This avoids wandering to bad regions. But local minima may require temporary increase; we can allow tolerance.

Implement:
```
for T in temps:
  z = z_best.copy(); m=v=0; t=0; no=0
  for it in range(max_iters):
    compute, update z; evaluate sc
    if sc < best_score: best=..., z_best=z.copy(); no=0
    elif sc > best_score * (1 + 0.002) and no > 500: no++? Actually no_improve counts steps since best, not only worse.
    if no > patience or scoring degraded too much (sc > best*1.02 for 100): break
```
But if we only update z by Adam, it may drift gradually; no_improve patience maybe 2000.

But F_T may not decrease actual. However, annealing T from low to high should progressively reduce actual. We can implement projected FISTA in a-space (simplex) for F_T? Pseudo:
```
def adam_lse(a0, time_limit, temps, max_iters_per_stage, lr0=0.2, patience=5000):
  best = score_fast(a0); best_z=log(a0)
  for T in temps:
    z=best_z.copy(); m=zeros; v=zeros; t=0; lr=lr0
    no_improve=0; last_best_time=time
    for it in range(max_iters_per_stage):
      if time out: break
      a=softmax(z); A=...; b=...
      sc=...
      if sc < best: best=...; best_z=z.copy(); no_improve=0; lr=min(lr*1.03, 2.0); best_time=now
      else: no_improve+=1
      # compute weights; if T == 0?? no.
      dz=...
      # if dz all tiny, break
      if no_improve > patience or (no_improve > 200 and sc > best*1.01): break
      # adaptive lr: if no_improve > 200 and no improvement for 500: lr*=0.9
      t+=1; update
    # after stage, continue
  return best_a
```

10. `optimize_direct(a0, time_budget, mode, T=None, K=None, lr=0.25, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, eps=1e-12, patience=5000, verbose=True)`:
   - `deadline = time.monotonic() + time_budget`.
   - n, M = precompute.
   - a = normalize_prob(a0), z = z_from_a(a) - mean.
   - best_a, best_score = score_fast(a)
   - m, v arrays; t=0; no_improve=0; last_improve=now.
   - `stage_lr = lr`
   - For iteration until deadline:
       - if time > deadline: break
       - a = softmax_from_z(z)
       - A = rfft(a, M)
       - b = irfft(A*A, M)[:2*n-1]
       - sc = 2*n*max(b)
       - if sc < best_score: best...; no_improve=0; if stage_lr < 1.0: stage_lr *= 1.02 maybe; last...
       else: no_improve +=1
       - if no_improve > patience: break (return best)
       - weights = compute_weights(b, mode, T, K)
       - if weights.sum() <= 0: break
       - grad_a = gradient_fft(a, weights, M) # actually uses A again; pass A to avoid recompute
       - dz = a*(grad_a - dot(grad_a,a))
       - norm = sqrt(mean(dz*dz)+eps); if norm<1e-20 break
       - dz /= norm
       - t +=1
       - m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*dz
       - v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*dz*dz
       - mhat = m/(1-beta1**t)
       - vhat = v/(1-beta2**t)
       - z -= stage_lr * mhat / (sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       - z -= z.mean()
       - if sc > best_score * 1.05 and no_improve > 1000: stage_lr*=0.9; maybe revert? We can set z = z_best? But z_best not stored per best? We can store z_best when best improves. At revert, z = best_z.copy(); m=v=0; t=0; stage_lr*=0.8; no_improve=0.
   - return best_a, best_score.

Procedure for `line_search_retreat(a0,time,mode,T,K,step0=0.1)`:
```
z=z_from_a; best=current; step=step0; fail_count=0
for it in range(max_iters):
  a=softmax(z); b=conv; sc=...
  if sc<best update; fail=0
  else fail++
  if fail>patience break
  weights=compute; grad...
  d = -a*(grad_a - dot)  # descent direction in z
  d_rmsscale? z change if add step*d. d has small magnitude; we need scale d to have RMS 1? If d raw RMS ~1e-10, step0 must be 1e9 to get 0.1. Instead normalize d: d = d / sqrt(mean(d^2)) so adding step is RMS z step. Then line search step around 0.1. But sign direction.
  if norm<1e-20 break
  d_norm = d/rms
  # Try steps descending: start with current step maybe, then 0.5,0.25...
  accepted=False
  for s in candidates [step, step*0.5,...]:
      z_trial = z - s*d_norm
      a_trial=softmax; sc_trial=score
      if sc_trial <= sc*(1+1e-10) or sc_trial < best:
          z=z_trial; step=min(s*1.1, max_step); accepted=True; break
  if not accepted:
      step*=0.5
      fail2++
      if step<min_step: break
      # maybe random jitter? 
```

Pseudo more concrete:
```
def optimize_direction(a0, time_budget, mode, T=None, K=None, max_step=1.0, min_step=1e-4, patience=2000):
    n=len(a0); M=...
    a=normalize_prob(a0); z=z_from_a(a); z-=z.mean()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a); best_z=z.copy()
    step=0.1; current_score=best_score; no_improve=0; stalled=0
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_budget
    while time<deadline:
       a=softmax_from_z(z)
       # compute current score and A,b
       A=rfft(a,M); b=irfft(A*A,M)[:2*n-1]; sc=2*n*b.max()
       current_score=sc
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_z=z.copy(); no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve += 1
       if no_improve > patience: break
       # weights
       w=compute_weights(b,mode,T,K)
       if w.sum()<=0: break
       grad=fft_gradient(A,w,M,n)
       d = a*(grad - np.dot(grad,a))
       # d is gradient in z (positive increases objective? grad of F wrt a; to decrease, -d)
       rms=sqrt(mean(d*d)+eps)
       if rms < 1e-20: break
       d = d/rms
       # line search: decreasing steps
       accepted=False
       # Try current step, then halves. Optionally if current step failed many, smaller.
       s=step
       min_allowed = min_step
       while s >= min_allowed:
          z_trial = z - s*d
          # optional jitter? no
          sc_trial = score_from_z(z_trial, M)  # computes softmax and conv
          if sc_trial <= current_score * (1.0 + 1e-9) or sc_trial < best_score:
              z = z_trial; current_score = sc_trial
              # update step: if improved, increase slightly; else keep/reduce
              if sc_trial < current_score_old? Need old before.
              accepted=True
              # adjust step:
              if sc_trial < best_score - 1e-12:
                  step = min(s * 1.1, max_step)
              elif s < step:
                  step = s * 1.0
              else:
                  step = min(s * 0.9, max_step)?
              break
          s *= 0.5
       if not accepted:
          step *= 0.5
          if step < min_step*0.5: break
          # maybe revert to best_z and add small random perturbation?
          if no_improve > 500:
              z = best_z.copy() + 0.01*randn? But deterministic? Use rng.
              current_score = best_score
              no_improve=0
              step = min_step*10
       # To avoid many small accepted steps that don't improve best, track no_improve? Already counts based on current sc after line search? We decrement only if current sc improves best? Wait no_improve increments every outer if current sc not new best. If small accepted steps not best, no_improve grows. Patience break.
```

Implement `sequence_from_first_conv(b_first)`:
```
def build_from_first_convolutions(coeff):
    # coeff length n, nonnegative; a0=sqrt(coeff[0])
    n=len(coeff)
    a=np.zeros(n)
    if coeff[0] <= 0: return None
    a0=np.sqrt(coeff[0]); a[0]=a0
    for k in range(1,n):
        s=0.0
        # sum_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i}
        # vectorized dot a[1:k] with a[k-1:0:-1]
        if k > 1:
            s = np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])
        a[k] = (coeff[k] - s) / (2*a0)
    if np.any(a < -1e-8): return None
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum(); if s<... return None
    return a/s
```

So formal sqrt **maximizes sum under the first n constraints b_k≤1**? At step k, a_k = (1 - sum previous products)/(2 a0). If a0 fixed 1, to make b_k≤1, max a_k is this. Since later constraints depend on a_k, but perhaps this greedy maximizes sum. Yes, formal sqrt is a natural extremal for first n coefficients. To increase sum further, impossible under first n constraints (if a0=1). But we can choose a0<1 to allow larger later terms? Since a0 in denominator; if a0 smaller, given b_k≤1, a_k can be larger (because denominator smaller), but a0^2 reduction may allow total sum larger? Let's test: If a0<c<1, then b0=c^2. Constraint b_k≤1. Recurrence to maximize a_k: a_k ≤ (1 - Σ prev)/(2c). Smaller c -> larger later a, potentially sum bigger. But b1=2c a1≤1 => a1≤1/(2c), which grows as c small. Sum may increase? For c→0, a1 huge, but later constraints? Need nonnegative and high-degree constraints. This is promising! Formal with c=1 may not maximize total mass under all constraints; a0 less than 1 could allow a tail mass, increasing sum^2/max and lowering score. Since objective normalized, scaling out max=1 (some b_lag=1), a0 can be <1 as long as max elsewhere =1. Indeed target max may be center or tail, not b0. So optimal likely has a0 < 1 relative to max.

We can formulate **maximize S(a) = sum a** subject to a≥0, max b_k =1. This is equivalent to minimizing score. This is a nonlinear programming problem but maybe we can solve via **bisection/multiplicative**. The constraints b_k≤1. The formal sqrt with a0=1 is one feasible with score π/2. We want larger S; maybe optimum S^2 such that score target 1.5 => S^2≈(2n)/1.5≈1.333n, while formal S^2≈1.273n (4/π=1.273). Need only 5% more sum. We can achieve by reducing a0 and adding right mass.

This suggests an algorithm: **Scale-invariant constraint programming**. Normalize such that a0 = t (maybe <1). For fixed t, to maximize sum under b_k≤1, we can greedily set a_k? Since constraints for k< n involve previous and a_k linearly: b_k = 2 t a_k + Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i} ≤1. Therefore maximum a_k = (1 - sum_prev)/(2t). To maximize total sum, choose a_k at its maximum for k=0..n-1. This determines a (first n) as a function of t (and earlier choices). This is exactly the formal sqrt scaled/generalized: a0=t, a_k=(1 - dot)/(2t). It may produce positive sequence. Then high coefficients b_n.. automatically? We can compute score. We can optimize over t! This is a one-dimensional family that parametrizes the **greedy maximal** sequences under first-half constraints. Formal t=1. t<1 may improve. This is extremely promising and easy!

Let's analyze recurrence with arbitrary t>0:
- a0=t.
- For k=1..n-1, let s_k = Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i}. Then max a_k satisfying b_k≤1 is (1 - s_k)/(2t), assuming nonnegative. If we choose this, all first n b_k exactly 1. This is like solving A(x)^2 coefficients 1 for k<n with a0=t. But as k increases, a_k may become negative if s_k>1. Need t not too small. The sequence may have positive values and perhaps a tail. The high b_k may be ≤1 or may exceed; score = 2n * max(1, max_tail) / S^2. If max_tail ≤1, score=2n/S^2. We want maximize S (or minimize score). We can sweep t to find optimum where a nonnegative and max_tail maybe 1.

This is a **generalized formal sqrt** with parameter t. Great! Let's test small: t=1 formal. t=0.9: a0=0.9; a1=(1)/(1.8)=0.5556; a2=(1 - a1^2)/(1.8)= (1-0.3086)/1.8=0.384; similar but a1 larger, a0 smaller; sum maybe? a0 sum decreases, a1.. increase. Overall sum may increase. Need ensure a_k positive. t maybe ~0.8? a1=0.625, a2=(1-0.3906)/1.6=0.381; a3? s=a1a2? ~0.238, a3=(1-0.238)/1.6=0.476; sequence might rise later, positive larger. Sum could be much larger. But high b tail may exceed 1 due larger later terms. The optimum likely where some high b becomes 1, balancing.

We can run a 1D sweep over t and evaluate score; then use t-optimal as initialization for full local optimization. This is a novel, cheap, powerful idea.

Let's implement `build_greedy(t, n)`:
```
a=np.zeros(n)
a[0]=t
for k in range(1,n):
    if k>1:
       s = np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])
    else: s=0
    a_k = (1 - s) / (2*t)
    if a_k < 0: return None (or clip and break)
    a[k]=a_k
# evaluate raw score: S=sum(a), maxb=max(conv(a,a)); score=2n*maxb/S^2
# Normalize returned but score invariant.
```
Complexity O(n^2). For n=1200, okay. For n=3000, 4.5M dot operations in C, maybe 0.05-0.2s per t. Sweeping 50 t -> several seconds. Good. We can also compute recurrence faster using FFT? Not needed.

What upper bound on t? If t too small, a_k may become large; sum may blow, but high b constraints may fail. We can allow a_k positive but score maybe high if maxb large. We'll compute.

We can vectorize the dot inside loop using preallocated? The dot slices in C are okay. But 50*720k=36M dot operations? Actually dot total per build ~n^2/2=720k multiplications, in C. 50 builds=36M, fine (<1s). For n=2000, 2M*50=100M, maybe 1s-2s.

We need choose t range. t positive; if t=1 formal. If t→? lower bound positivity. We can initial scan t from e.g. 0.1 to 2.0. But a0=t, if t>1, b0=t^2>1, max>1; relative max maybe t^2; formula always evaluate actual. t large maybe bad. We can normalize in raw units not enforce max=1; the recurrence used threshold 1 for b_k constraints but if t not 1, first n b_k =1 still except b0=t^2? Wait for k=0, we set a0=t not necessarily satisfying b0=1. The recurrence ensured b_k=1 for k=1..n-1, while b0=t^2. If t<1, b0<1; if t>1, b0>1. If t<1, max may be tail or 1; if t>1, max at b0. But if t too small, a_k may become negative before n. We catch and skip.

This one-parameter family might by itself achieve near 1.5027! Then local polish can get below.

       # if time...

We can implement `optimize_adam(a0, time_budget, mode, T, K, lr=0.05, max_step=0.5, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, patience=5000)`:
- current z; best_z.
- m,v zero; t=0; step=lr.
- Each iter:
   - a=softmax(z); compute A,b,sc.
   - if sc < best: update best_z, no=0, step=min(step*1.05,max_step)
   - else no++
   - weights, grad, dz = a*(grad - dot)
   - scale_dz = max(abs(dz)); if scale <1e-20 break; dz/=scale

   - cap delta to max_step
   - z_new = z - delta
   - z_new -= z_new.mean()
   - # Optional: if expected bad, we don't check score until next loop. If no improves for 500 and sc current > best*1.01, revert to best_z, reset m/v, step*=0.7.
   - z = z_new
- This can take a step that increases score a lot; next loop detects and maybe reverts. Fine.

def next_pow2(x): return 1 << (int(max(1,x))-1).bit_length()
```

Implementation of `optimize_adam`:
```
def optimize_adam(a0, time_budget, mode, T=None, K=None, max_iter=1000000, lr=0.05, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, max_step=0.5, patience=3000, verbose=False):
    n=len(a0); M=next_pow2(3*n-1); L=2*n-1
    z=z_from_a(a0); z-=z.mean()
    best_a=a0.copy(); best_score=score_fast_M(a0,M,n)
    best_z=z.copy()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    step=lr
    no_improve=0; since_revert=0
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_budget
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       z = z - z.mean()? already
       a=softmax_from_z(z)
       A=rfft(a,M)
       b=irfft(A*A,M)[:L]
       maxb=b.max()
       sc=2.0*n*maxb
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_z=z.copy(); no_improve=0; since_revert=0
           step = min(step*1.03, max_step)
       else:
           no_improve +=1; since_revert +=1
       if no_improve > patience: break
       if since_revert > 2000 and sc > best_score*1.01:
           z=best_z.copy(); m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=0; step *=0.7; no_improve=0; since_revert=0
           continue
       w=compute_weights(b,mode,T,K)
       if w.sum() <= 1e-300: break
       V=rfft(w[::-1], M)
       grad=2.0*np.real(irfft(A*V, M))
       grad=grad[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]  # length n? ensure
       # Numerical: if n=1, handle
       dz = a * (grad - np.dot(grad,a))
       scale = np.max(np.abs(dz))
       if scale < 1e-20: break
       dz = dz / scale
       t +=1
       m = beta1*m + (1.0-beta1)*dz
       v = beta2*v + (1.0-beta2)*dz*dz
       mhat = m/(1.0-beta1**t)
       vhat = v/(1.0-beta2**t)
       delta = step * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
       # cap
       delta = np.clip(delta, -max_step, max_step)
       z = z - delta
       z = z - z.mean()
       # optional periodic center and clip z to prevent extremes
       z = np.clip(z, -50, 50)
       it +=1
       if verbose and it%2000==0: print...
    return best_a, best_score
```
Note: `grad` slice for n: `grad[2*n-2:n-2:-1]`. For n=1, stop negative? Let's handle n=1 separately: b=[a^2], score=2, no optimization. Not needed.
Check segment length: start=2n-2, stop=n-2 exclusive, step -1 gives n values: indices 2n-2, ..., n-1. Good.
Potential issue: `irfft(A*V, M)` length M, but linear correlation length n+L-1=3n-2. M>=3n-1? `next_pow2(3*n-1)` gives M ≥3n-1, last index 3n-2? Wait length needed is n+L-1 = n+2n-1-1? Convolution of a length n and w_rev length L length n+L-1 = 3n-2. Max index 3n-3. Our segment max index 2n-2, okay. M>=3n-1 maybe more than enough. For n=2, 3n-2=4, M=4? 3n-1=5 -> next 8, but M=4 enough? next_pow2(3*n-1) with n=2 -> next_pow2(5)=8; overkill. Could use `next_pow2(3*n-2)`. For n=1200, 3598 ->4096; 3n-1=3600 ->4096. Same. Use `max_len = 3*n-2; M=next_pow2(max_len)`. For n=1, M=1.

`irfft(A*A,M)[:L]` linear conv length 2n-1 ≤M.

Compute weights: if mode 'lse', we use all b. We can avoid exp overflow:
```
abs_T = abs(T) if T else 0
if abs_T == 0: return uniform
maxb=np.max(b)
z=T*(b-maxb)
# If min z < -745, exp underflows to 0 okay (np.exp handles)
e=np.exp(z)
s=e.sum()
if not finite or s==0: return topk uniform
return e/s
```
If T negative? not.
For 'poly': threshold fraction `thr = 0.0 if T is None/abs?`. We'll use T as float threshold fraction. `mean = 1.0/L` because sum b=1. But due numerical sum maybe not 1; use `b_sum = b.sum(); mean = b_sum/L` (should 1). `w = b - thr*mean`; clip. If w.sum small, use b.
For 'topk': K int. If K is None or K<=0: uniform? For topk stage, K maybe 100. Use `idx = np.argpartition(b, min(K,L)-1)[:K]` if K<L else all. `w=zeros; w[idx]=1.0; return w`. If K=L, uniform.

Now, `optimize_adam_multi(a0, time_budget, specs, verbose)`:
```
best_a=a0; best_score=score_fast(a0)
for spec in specs:
  if time > deadline: break
  remaining=time_budget - elapsed

Let's write code details carefully.

`normalize_prob`:
```
def normalize_prob(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    return a/s
```

`interpolate_sequence`:
```
def interpolate_sequence(f, new_n):
    f=np.asarray(f,dtype=np.float64)
    if f.size == new_n: return normalize_prob(f)
    old_n=f.size
    if old_n <=1: return np.full(new_n, 1.0/new_n)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old_n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
    val=np.interp(x_new,x_old,f)
    return normalize_prob(val)
```

`score_fast` with optional M:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-15: return inf
    M=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    b=fast_convolve(a)
    maxb=b.max()
    return float(2.0*n*maxb/(s*s))
```
For normalized sum=1, same. In line search, use score_fast_M:
```
def score_fast_M(a, M, n):
    A=rfft(a, M)
    b=irfft(A*A, M)[:2*n-1]
    return float(2.0*n*b.max())
```
Assumes sum=1. If not, could scale. We'll ensure normalized.

`fast_convolve`:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1; M=next_pow2(L)
    A=rfft(a,M); return irfft(A*A,M)[:L]
```

`next_pow2`:
```
def next_pow2(x):
    x=int(max(1,x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()
```

`softmax_from_z`:
```
def softmax_from_z(z):
    z=z - np.max(z)
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=e.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
       return np.ones(z.size)/z.size
    return e/s
```

`z_from_a`:
```
def z_from_a(a):
    return np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
```

`compute_weights`:
Need L length, n optional? Use mean from b sum.
```
def compute_weights(b, mode, param, K):
    L=b.size
    if mode == 'lse':
       T=float(param)
       if T <=0: return np.ones(L)/L
       maxb=np.max(b)
       z=T*(b-maxb)
       # prevent overflow: z max 0, min maybe -inf
       e=np.exp(z)
       s=float(np.sum(e))
       if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
           # fallback top 10
           k=min(10,L)
           idx=np.argpartition(b, k-1)[-k:]
           w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0; return w
       return e/s
    if mode == 'poly':
       thr=0.0 if param is None else float(param)
       bs=float(np.sum(b))
       mean=bs/L if bs>0 else 1.0/L
       w=b - thr*mean
       w=np.maximum(w,0.0)
       s=float(np.sum(w))
       if s <= 1e-300:
           w=b.copy()
           s=float(np.sum(w))
           if s>0: w=w/s
       return w
    if mode == 'topk':
       if K is None or K<=0 or K>=L:
           return np.ones(L)/L
       idx=np.argpartition(b, K-1)[:K]
       w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0
       return w
    return np.ones(L)/L
```
For poly, returning unnormalized w; gradient scale normalized later. It might be large but okay. If we scaled, no matter.

`fft_gradient`:
```
def fft_gradient(A, w, M, n):
    if n == 1:
       return np.array([2.0 * (w[0] if w.size>0 else 0.0)])
    V = rfft(w[::-1], M)
    corr = np.real(irfft(A * V, M))
    return 2.0 * corr[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]
```
Check `A` length M complex; V complex. Good.

Potential problem: `rfft(w[::-1], M)` each iteration recomputes; w is float. Fine.

`optimize_adam` code: Need to return best_z? Not necessary. But for line search final starting from best, best_a enough. We don't need z.
Implement with time_budget and mode param.
```
def optimize_adam(a0, time_budget, mode, param, K, lr=0.05, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, max_step=0.5, patience=5000, verbose=False):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=min(start+time_budget, _global_deadline-1.0)
    a=normalize_prob(a0); n=a.size; L=2*n-1; M=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    z=z_from_a(a); z-=z.mean()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast_M(a,M,n)
    best_z=z.copy()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; step=lr; no_improve=0; bad_count=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       a=softmax_from_z(z)
       A=rfft(a,M)
       b=irfft(A*A,M)[:L]
       maxb=float(np.max(b))
       sc=2.0*n*maxb
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_z=z.copy(); no_improve=0; bad_count=0
          if step < max_step: step = min(step*1.03, max_step)
       else:
          no_improve += 1; bad_count += 1
       if no_improve > patience:
          break
       if bad_count > 3000 and sc > best_score*1.02:
          z=best_z.copy(); m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=0; step*=0.7; no_improve=0; bad_count=0
          if step < 1e-4: break
          continue
       if no_improve > 1000 and sc > best_score*1.005:
          step *= 0.9
       w=compute_weights(b, mode, param, K)
       ws=float(np.sum(w))
       if ws <= 1e-300:
          break
       V=rfft(w[::-1], M)
       grad=fft_gradient_from_corr(A,V,M,n) # helper maybe
       # We already computed V, avoid fft_gradient re-rfft w
```
Let's create `gradient_from_A_and_V(A,V,M,n)` to avoid re-rfft.

def gradient_from_A_V(A,V,M,n):

```
In optimize, `V = rfft(w[::-1], M); grad = gradient_from_A_V(A,V,M,n)`.

`optimize_line`:
```
def optimize_line(a0, time_budget, mode, param, K, max_step=0.2, min_step=1e-5, patience=2000, verbose=False):
  start... deadline...
  z=z_from_a(a0); z=normalize_z(z)
  best_a=a0; best_score=score_fast_M
  step=max_step; since_best=0; fail=0
  while now<deadline:
    a=softmax(z); A=... b... sc
    if sc < best_score: update; since_best=0; fail=0
    else: since_best+=1
    if since_best > patience: break
    w=compute...
    grad...
    dz=...
    scale=max_abs; if small break; d=d/scale
    s=step; accepted=False; old=sc
    # To save, try at most 4 steps: current, half, quarter, eighth
    for factor in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
       trial_s = s*factor
       if trial_s < min_step: break
       z_trial=normalize_z(z - trial_s*d)
       sc_trial=score_from_z(z_trial,M,n)
       if sc_trial <= old*(1+1e-9) or sc_trial < best_score*(1-1e-12):
          z=z_trial; sc=sc_trial; accepted=True
          # adjust step
          if sc_trial < old*(1-1e-6): step = min(trial_s*1.1, max_step)
          else: step = min(trial_s, max_step)
          if sc_trial < best_score: best...; since_best=0
          break
    if not accepted:
       step *=0.5
       fail +=1
       if step < min_step: break
       if fail > 20:
          z=best_z if stored? we didn't store; store best_z.
```

```
def run_stages(a, total, specs, label):
    best = normalize_prob(a); best_sc=score_fast(best)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=min(start+total, _global_deadline-10)
    for spec in specs:
       mode,param,K,frac,lr = spec
       spec_time = min(frac, deadline-time.monotonic())
       if spec_time < 5: break
       out, sc = optimize_adam(best, spec_time, mode, param, K, lr=lr)
       if sc < best_sc:
          best=out; best_sc=sc
          print(label, mode, sc, time)
    return best, best_sc
```

Let's implement `build_greedy` with a local variable for speed:
```
def build_greedy(t, n):
    a=np.zeros(n)
    if t <=1e-8: return None
    a[0]=t
    a0=t
    for k in range(1,n):
        if k > 1:
           s=float(np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1]))
        else: s=0.0
        val=(1.0 - s)/(2.0*a0)
        if val < -1e-8:
           return None
        if val < 0.0: val=0.0
        a[k]=val
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=1e-15: return None
    return a/s
```
Note: For t very small, val may be huge; convolution score maybe overflow? Values sum scaled to 1 after; raw a values can be large if t small and s<1; e.g., t=0.52, a1≈0.96, a2 maybe small; not huge. If t near 0.51, a1~0.98, a2? maybe negative. Fine.

Can we improve build by using FFT to compute s_k = coefficient of convolution of a with a at k? s_k = Σ_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i} = b_k - 2a0 a_k? Circular. But we don't know a_k. Could update via FFT if we had previous? The recurrence sequential due a_k appears in b_k linearly; no need FFT.

def add_candidate(candidates, seq):
    if seq is None: return
    a=normalize_prob(seq)
    if a.size == 0: return
    try: sc=score_fast(a)
    except: sc=float('inf')
    if np.isfinite(sc): candidates.append((sc, a.copy()))
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800.0):
    global _global_deadline
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    total_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 45.0)
    _global_deadline = total_deadline
    candidates=[]
    parent=None
    try: ...
    except: pass
    if parent is not None: add_candidate(candidates,parent)
    else: # add formal sqrt
        a=build_greedy(1.0,1200); add_candidate(candidates,a)
    n_base = len(parent) if parent is not None else 1200
    # candidate lengths
    lengths=[]
    for add in [0, 300, 600, 1200, 1800]:
       n=n_base+add
       if 400 <= n <= 3000:
          if n not in lengths: lengths.append(n)
    # t-sweeps
    sweep_deadline = min(_global_deadline - 300, time.monotonic() + 250.0)
    for n in lengths:
       if time.monotonic() > sweep_deadline: break
       init=None
       if parent is not None: init=interpolate_sequence(parent,n); add_candidate(candidates, init)
       a_t, sc_t = sweep_t(n, sweep_deadline)
       add_candidate(candidates, a_t)
       print(f"T-sweep n={n} score={sc_t:.8f}")
    # perturb parent/top candidates
    # Add some structured/perturbed candidates
    if parent is not None:
       for scale in [0.02,0.05]:
           a=normalize_prob(parent + scale*rng.standard_normal(parent.size))

    # Also add formal t for base length from build if not
    a=build_greedy(0.85,n_base); add_candidate(candidates,a)
    a=build_greedy(1.0,n_base); add_candidate(candidates,a)
    # Pair different lengths? maybe reverse? no.
    # local polishing

    polished_lengths=set()

    # main polish best

          add_candidate(candidates,out)
          print("main polished", sc)
    # second candidate different length maybe

       remaining = _global_deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining < 100: break
       seq=candidates[idx][1]
       if len(seq) in polished_lengths: continue
       t_sec=min(300.0, remaining*0.3)
       if t_sec < 60: break
       out,sc=run_small(seq, t_sec, "second")
       add_candidate(candidates,out)

    # third shorter?

       seq = min(candidates, key? no lambda) candidates sorted? Use sorted list and pick first not polished maybe.
```

    start=time.monotonic()
    L=2*n-1; M=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    best_a=None; best_score=inf; best_t=1.0

       if a is None: continue
       sc=score_fast_M(a,M,n)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_score: ...

Let's double-check the gradient formula for standard convolution `np.convolve(a,a)`. For b_k=Σ_i a_i a_{k-i}. Derivative wrt a_j: terms where i=j: a_{k-j}; where k-i=j: a_j? Wait second term is a_j (since a_j in second factor). If both i=j and k-i=j (j=k/2), the term a_j^2 appears once in sum, derivative 2a_j. Our formula gave 2 a_{k-j} if valid. For k=1, j=0: 2a1, correct; j=1:2a0. For k=2, j=1:2a1, correct. For k=0, j=0:2a0. Good. So gradient=2 shift, not sum of two shifts separately. ```python
"""
Minimizes evaluate_sequence for non-negative step-height sequences.

The main ideas are:
1. A cheap one-parameter family of strong candidates built from the formal
   square root of a flat convolution profile, swept over the endpoint scale.
2. Fast log-space Adam refinement using Fourier-based gradients of
   LogSumExp / active-peak surrogates for the convolution peak.
3. A safe line-search finisher in log-space that only accepts steps which do
   not worsen the convolution peak.
4. Multiple lengths are explored, and the final answer is chosen using the
   actual evaluate_sequence oracle.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
from operator import itemgetter

_global_deadline = 0.0


def next_pow2(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_prob(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def fast_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    M = next_pow2(L)
    A = rfft(a, M)
    return np.asarray(irfft(A * A, M)[:L], dtype=np.float64)


def score_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float('inf')
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return float('inf')
    b = fast_convolve(a)
    mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float('inf')
    return float(2.0 * n * mb / (s * s))


def score_fast_M(a, M, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    A = rfft(a, M)
    b = np.asarray(irfft(A * A, M)[:2 * n - 1], dtype=np.float64)
    mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float('inf')
    return float(2.0 * n * mb)


def softmax_from_z(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(z.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(z.size)
    return e / s


def z_from_a(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    return np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))


def normalize_z(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.clip(z, -60.0, 60.0)
    z = z - float(np.mean(z))
    return z


def interpolate_sequence(f, new_n):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f.size == 0:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)
    if f.size == new_n:
        return normalize_prob(f)
    if f.size == 1:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, f.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n)
    val = np.interp(x_new, x_old, f)
    return normalize_prob(val)


def evaluate_safe(seq):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        lst = [float(x) for x in arr]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')


def add_candidate(candidates, seq, max_keep=24):
    if seq is None:
        return
    try:
        a = normalize_prob(seq)
        if a.size == 0:
            return
        sc = score_fast(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((sc, a.copy()))
            if len(candidates) > max_keep:
                candidates.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
                del candidates[max_keep:]
    except Exception:
        pass


def build_greedy(t, n):
    """
    Builds a sequence from the recurrence determined by a0 = t and
    b_k ~= 1 for k >= 1 through k < n.  This is a one-parameter family
    near the formal square root of a flat convolution profile.
    """
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    t = float(t)
    if not np.isfinite(t) or t <= 1e-10:
        return None

    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = t
    a0 = t

    for k in range(1, n):
        if k > 1:
            s = float(np.dot(a[1:k], a[k - 1:0:-1]))
        else:
            s = 0.0
        val = (1.0 - s) / (2.0 * a0)
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            return None
        if val < 0.0:
            # Small negative values may be caused by roundoff.  For larger
            # negative values we still clip, producing a heuristic candidate
            # that may or may not be useful after normalization.
            val = 0.0
        a[k] = val

    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return a / s


def sweep_t(n, deadline):
    L = 2 * n - 1
    M = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    best_a = None
    best_score = float('inf')
    best_t = 1.0

    base = np.geomspace(0.56, 1.25, 26)
    extra = np.array([0.60, 0.68, 0.75, 0.80, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95, 1.00, 1.05, 1.10])
    ts = np.unique(np.concatenate([base, extra]))

    for tt in ts:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        a = build_greedy(float(tt), n)
        if a is None:
            continue
        sc = score_fast_M(a, M, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_t = float(tt)

    if best_a is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        lo = max(0.53, best_t * 0.94)
        hi = min(1.30, best_t * 1.06)
        if hi > lo + 1e-12:
            ts2 = np.linspace(lo, hi, 14)
            for tt in ts2:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                a = build_greedy(float(tt), n)
                if a is None:
                    continue
                sc = score_fast_M(a, M, n)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = a.copy()
                    best_t = float(tt)

    return best_a, best_score


def compute_weights(b, mode, param, K):
    L = b.size

    if mode == "lse":
        T = float(param)
        if T <= 0.0:
            return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
        maxb = float(np.max(b))
        z = T * (b - maxb)
        e = np.exp(z)
        s = float(np.sum(e))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            k = min(20, L)
            if k <= 0:
                return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
            idx = np.argpartition(b, k - 1)[-k:]
            w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
            w[idx] = 1.0
            return w
        return e / s

    if mode == "poly":
        thr = 0.0 if param is None else float(param)
        bs = float(np.sum(b))
        mean = bs / float(L) if bs > 0.0 else 1.0 / float(L)
        w = b - thr * mean
        w = np.maximum(w, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(w))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            w = b.copy()
            s = float(np.sum(w))
            if s > 0.0:
                w = w / s
        return w

    if mode == "topk":
        if K is None or K <= 0 or K >= L:
            return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
        idx = np.argpartition(b, int(K) - 1)[:int(K)]
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w

    return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)


def gradient_from_A_V(A, V, M, n):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        # For n=1 the gradient is not used by the optimizers.
        return np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)
    corr = np.real(irfft(A * V, M))
    return 2.0 * corr[2 * n - 2 : n - 2 : -1]


def score_from_z(z, M, n):
    a = softmax_from_z(z)
    A = rfft(a, M)
    b = np.asarray(irfft(A * A, M)[:2 * n - 1], dtype=np.float64)
    mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float('inf')
    return float(2.0 * n * mb)


def optimize_adam(a0, time_budget, mode, param, K,
                  lr=0.05, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999,
                  max_step=0.5, patience=5000, verbose=False):
    a = normalize_prob(a0)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return a.copy(), score_fast(a)

    M = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = min(start + float(time_budget), _global_deadline - 2.0)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return a.copy(), score_fast(a)

    z = normalize_z(z_from_a(a))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast_M(a, M, n)
    best_z = z.copy()

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    step = float(lr)
    no_improve = 0
    bad_count = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        a = softmax_from_z(z)
        A = rfft(a, M)
        b = np.asarray(irfft(A * A, M)[:L], dtype=np.float64)
        sc = 2.0 * n * float(np.max(b))

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_z = z.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            bad_count = 0
            step = min(step * 1.03, max_step)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            bad_count += 1

        if no_improve > patience:
            break

        if bad_count > 3000 and sc > best_score * 1.02:
            z = best_z.copy()
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            t = 0
            step *= 0.7
            no_improve = 0
            bad_count = 0
            if step < 1e-4:
                break
            continue

        if no_improve > 1000 and sc > best_score * 1.005:
            step *= 0.9

        w = compute_weights(b, mode, param, K)
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 1e-300:
            break

        V = rfft(w[::-1], M)
        grad = gradient_from_A_V(A, V, M, n)
        if grad.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            break

        dz = a * (grad - float(np.dot(grad, a)))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(dz)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-20:
            break
        dz = dz / scale

        t += 1
        m = beta1 * m + (1.0 - beta1) * dz
        v = beta2 * v + (1.0 - beta2) * dz * dz
        mhat = m / (1.0 - beta1 ** t)
        vhat = v / (1.0 - beta2 ** t)
        delta = step * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-12)
        delta = np.clip(delta, -max_step, max_step)

        z = z - delta
        z = normalize_z(z)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
            z = best_z.copy()
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            t = 0
            step *= 0.8
            if step < 1e-4:
                break
            continue

        it += 1
        if verbose and it % 5000 == 0:
            print(f"    adam {mode} iter={it} cur={sc:.8f} best={best_score:.8f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def optimize_line(a0, time_budget, mode, param, K,
                  max_step=0.15, min_step=1e-5, patience=1500, verbose=False):
    a = normalize_prob(a0)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return a.copy(), score_fast(a)

    M = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = min(start + float(time_budget), _global_deadline - 2.0)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return a.copy(), score_fast(a)

    z = normalize_z(z_from_a(a))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast_M(a, M, n)
    best_z = z.copy()

    step = float(max_step)
    since_best = 0
    fail = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        a = softmax_from_z(z)
        A = rfft(a, M)
        b = np.asarray(irfft(A * A, M)[:L], dtype=np.float64)
        sc = 2.0 * n * float(np.max(b))
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            z = best_z.copy()
            continue

        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_z = z.copy()
            since_best = 0
            fail = 0

        since_best += 1
        if since_best > patience:
            break

        w = compute_weights(b, mode, param, K)
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 1e-300:
            break

        V = rfft(w[::-1], M)
        grad = gradient_from_A_V(A, V, M, n)
        if grad.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            break

        dz = a * (grad - float(np.dot(grad, a)))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(dz)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-20:
            break
        d = dz / scale

        accepted = False
        old_sc = sc

        for fac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
            trial_s = step * fac
            if trial_s < min_step:
                break
            z_trial = normalize_z(z - trial_s * d)
            sc_trial = score_from_z(z_trial, M, n)
            if np.isfinite(sc_trial) and (sc_trial <= old_sc * (1.0 + 1e-9)):
                z = z_trial
                sc = sc_trial
                accepted = True
                if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = softmax_from_z(z)
                    best_a = normalize_prob(best_a)
                    best_z = z.copy()
                    since_best = 0
                    fail = 0
                if sc < old_sc * (1.0 - 1e-6):
                    step = min(trial_s * 1.08, max_step)
                else:
                    step = min(trial_s, max_step)
                break

        if not accepted:
            step *= 0.5
            fail += 1
            if step < min_step:
                break
            if fail > 25:
                z = best_z.copy()
                step = max(0.05, min(0.15, step))
                fail = 0
                since_best = 0

        it += 1
        if verbose and it % 1000 == 0:
            print(f"    line {mode} iter={it} cur={sc:.8f} best={best_score:.8f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def full_specs(total):
    total = float(total)
    return [
        ("poly", 0.00, 0, 0.11 * total, 0.055),
        ("poly", 0.25, 0, 0.13 * total, 0.055),
        ("lse", 1000.0, 0, 0.15 * total, 0.045),
        ("poly", 0.50, 0, 0.13 * total, 0.045),
        ("lse", 5000.0, 0, 0.16 * total, 0.035),
        ("lse", 20000.0, 0, 0.15 * total, 0.025),
        ("topk", 0.0, 220, 0.10 * total, 0.022),
        ("lse", 60000.0, 0, 0.07 * total, 0.018),
    ]


def small_specs(total):
    total = float(total)
    return [
        ("poly", 0.20, 0, 0.24 * total, 0.055),
        ("lse", 3500.0, 0, 0.24 * total, 0.04),
        ("poly", 0.60, 0, 0.18 * total, 0.035),
        ("lse", 18000.0, 0, 0.18 * total, 0.025),
        ("topk", 0.0, 160, 0.16 * total, 0.022),
    ]


def run_stages(a, total, specs, label):
    a = normalize_prob(a)
    if a.size <= 1:
        return a.copy(), score_fast(a)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = min(start + float(total), _global_deadline - 5.0)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)

    for spec in specs:
        mode, param, K, frac, lr = spec
        spec_time = min(float(frac), deadline - time.monotonic())
        if spec_time < 4.0:
            break
        if time.monotonic() >= _global_deadline - 20.0:
            break
        out, out_score = optimize_adam(best, spec_time, mode, param, K, lr=float(lr))
        if np.isfinite(out_score) and out_score < best_score:
            best = out
            best_score = out_score
        print(f"    [{label}] mode={mode} param={param} K={K} best={best_score:.8f}")

    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800.0):
    global _global_deadline

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    total_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 45.0)
    _global_deadline = total_deadline

    candidates = []
    parent = None

    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)) and float(np.sum(p)) > 1e-8:
            parent = p / float(np.sum(p))
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        add_candidate(candidates, parent)
        n_base = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n_base = 1200
        add_candidate(candidates, build_greedy(1.0, n_base))

    print(f"parent initialized, n_base={n_base}")

    # Candidate lengths.  Larger lengths usually help a little but cost more.
    lengths = []
    for add in (0, 300, 600, 1200, 1800):
        nn = n_base + add
        if 400 <= nn <= 3000 and nn not in lengths:
            lengths.append(nn)

    sweep_deadline = min(_global_deadline - 320.0, time.monotonic() + 260.0)
    for nn in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() >= sweep_deadline:
            break
        if parent is not None:
            add_candidate(candidates, interpolate_sequence(parent, nn))
        a_t, sc_t = sweep_t(nn, sweep_deadline)
        add_candidate(candidates, a_t)
        print(f"T-sweep n={nn}: {sc_t:.8f}")

    # Add a few simple structured/perturbed seeds.
    for tt in (0.75, 0.85, 0.95, 1.05):
        add_candidate(candidates, build_greedy(tt, n_base))

    if parent is not None:
        for scale in (0.02, 0.05, 0.08):
            try:
                noise = scale * rng.standard_normal(parent.size)
                add_candidate(candidates, parent + noise)
            except Exception:
                pass

    candidates.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    print(f"seed candidates: {len(candidates)}, best fast={candidates[0][0] if candidates else float('inf'):.8f}")

    # Polish the strongest seeds.
    top_seeds = [seq.copy() for _, seq in candidates[:5]]

    polished = 0
    for idx, seq in enumerate(top_seeds):
        remaining = _global_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 70.0:
            break
        if polished == 0:
            stage_total = min(720.0, remaining * 0.55)
            specs = full_specs(stage_total)
            label = "main"
        elif polished == 1:
            stage_total = min(340.0, remaining * 0.34)
            specs = small_specs(stage_total)
            label = "alt1"
        else:
            stage_total = min(170.0, remaining * 0.22)
            specs = small_specs(stage_total)
            label = f"alt{polished}"

        if stage_total < 35.0:
            break

        out, out_score = run_stages(seq, stage_total, specs, label)
        add_candidate(candidates, out)
        candidates.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
        polished += 1
        print(f"polished {label}: {out_score:.8f}")

    # Safe final line-search finisher on the current best/fast-top candidates.
    remaining = _global_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 30.0:
        candidates.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
        best_seq = candidates[0][1].copy()

        t1 = min(120.0, remaining * 0.45)
        if t1 > 18.0:
            out, out_score = optimize_line(
                best_seq, t1, "poly", 0.65, 0,
                max_step=0.08, min_step=1e-5, patience=800, verbose=False
            )
            add_candidate(candidates, out)
            print(f"finish poly: {out_score:.8f}")

        remaining = _global_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 20.0:
            candidates.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
            best_seq = candidates[0][1].copy()
            t2 = min(90.0, remaining * 0.40)
            if t2 > 12.0:
                out, out_score = optimize_line(
                    best_seq, t2, "lse", 30000.0, 0,
                    max_step=0.06, min_step=1e-6, patience=500, verbose=False
                )
                add_candidate(candidates, out)
                print(f"finish lse: {out_score:.8f}")

    # Final verification with the actual evaluator.
    candidates.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    best_seq = None
    best_actual = float('inf')

    for score, seq in candidates[:12]:
        try:
            actual = evaluate_safe(seq)
        except Exception:
            actual = float('inf')
        if np.isfinite(actual) and actual < best_actual:
            best_actual = actual
            best_seq = seq.copy()
        print(f"evaluate actual for fast={score:.8f}: {actual:.8f}")

    if best_seq is None:
        if candidates:
            best_seq = candidates[0][1].copy()
        else:
            best_seq = build_greedy(1.0, 1200)

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(1200, dtype=np.float64) / 1200.0

    best_seq = normalize_prob(best_seq)
    print(f"FINAL fast={score_fast(best_seq):.10f}, n={best_seq.size}")
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```
